FILE: README.md

# Provenance: Elite Force II decomp (Android/Linux)

A native Android and Linux port of *Star Trek: Voyager — Elite Force II* (Ritual
Entertainment, 2003). The engine descends from id Tech 3 through
[OpenMoHAA](https://github.com/openmoh/openmohaa), with a from-scratch Vulkan
renderer written for this port. The game runs on your own retail installation —
**no game data is included**, and the DLL that ships with the game is used only
as a proof-of-ownership check. It is never executed.

A follow-up to my own Android ports —
[Voyager Elite Force: SP for Android](https://github.com/imjustadudegamer/VoyagerSP-Android)
(the first game) and
[Klingon Honor Guard: Android](https://github.com/imjustadudegamer/Klingon-Honor-GUARD-Android) —
and inspired by [VoyagerNX](https://github.com/faithvoid/VoyagerNX), faithvoid's Nintendo
Switch port of the first game.

![Gameplay on Android, with the touch controls](docs/img/gameplay-720p.png)

## Why "Provenance"?

Every function, struct, table and constant in this port is held to a witness.
Where the original source was published, that source is the authority: id
Software's GPL engine release, and Ritual's own Elite Force II fgame code.
Everything else is read out of the shipping retail binaries, the little known Mac
PPC release first and the PC build as a secondary corroboration, and every value
is checked against the retail data that ships with the game. Behaviour is not
assumed from the binaries alone: the retail game itself is instrumented and
measured, and port behaviour is diffed against retail's own output. A claim that
cannot be tied to one of those is recorded as open rather than asserted. The
name says that the code carries its own evidence chain. Ask where a behaviour
comes from and there is an answer.

## Features

- Vulkan renderer, written for this port — the shipping default. The GLES 3.2
  twin ships beside it and is selectable in Graphics → Renderer.
- Touch controls: virtual stick, look area, and on-screen pills for fire, jump,
  crouch, use, night vision and the tricorder.
- Full gamepad support (SDL GameController, EF1-style layout — see the table below),
  sharing one key map with the touch controls.
- First-run game import through the system file picker — no storage permission needed.
- Widescreen support for the 3D view, the HUD and the 2D menus.
- Android 8.0+ (`arm64-v8a`, 16 KB page-size compatible) and Linux x86-64 from one tree.

## Requirements

- Android 8.0+ with a Vulkan driver, or Linux x86-64 with a Vulkan driver.
- Your own retail *Elite Force II* data: the game's `.pk3` paks and the game DLL.

## Install

### Android

1. Install the APK from Releases and launch it.
2. On first run, pick your retail paks and the game DLL in the file picker
   (multi-select). The game copies them into place and starts.
3. Manual placement, if you prefer:

```sh
adb push base/. /sdcard/Android/obb/com.provenanceef2/base/
```

### Linux

1. Download `ProvenanceEF2-<version>-x86_64.AppImage` from Releases, make it
   executable and run it.
2. On first run, a dialog asks for your retail installation folder — the one
   holding `EF2.exe` and its `base/` directory. The choice is remembered;
   nothing is copied.
3. Point the game yourself, if you prefer:

```sh
./ProvenanceEF2-<version>-x86_64.AppImage +set fs_basepath /path/to/elite-force-ii
```

or drop the retail `base/` directory into `~/.local/share/provenanceef2/`,
where the engine looks on its own. Configs and saves land in the same tree.
The Linux release is Vulkan only.

## Controls

Gamepad (EF1-style layout, remappable in the in-game menu):

| Input | Action |
|---|---|
| Left stick | Move (click: walk) |
| Right stick | Look (click: aim) |
| Right trigger | Fire |
| Left trigger | Alt-fire |
| Right shoulder | Next inventory item |
| Left shoulder | Previous inventory item |
| A | Jump |
| B | Crouch |
| X | Use |
| Y | Night vision |
| D-pad up | Reload |
| D-pad down | Context action (`+button3`) |
| D-pad left/right | Lean |
| Back | Objectives |
| Start | Menu |

Touch: virtual stick, look area, and the on-screen pills (fire, jump, crouch,
use, night vision, tricorder). Keyboard and mouse keep the retail layout
(WASD, mouse to aim and fire, wheel for inventory, `F6`/`F9` quick-save/load,
`n` night vision, `1`–`6` weapon select).

## Known issues

This port is not fully play tested and the campaign has not been played end to
end; expect the occasional stutter while a new effect compiles. The graphics
have been through a retail-diff pass — the Borg generator effects (plasma rings
and the sparkle dome), lens flares (including the through-wall bug), phaser
scorch marks, decal fade and lifetime, ship trails, beam shapes, the additive
light blend and the view bob are all checked against retail's own output — so
almost all of the graphical bugs reported here are fixed. What is still open:

- **No stencil shadows** — `cg_shadows 2` draws nothing on this renderer, so the
  character shadows are the simple set (`cg_shadows 1`, the default).
- **A few effects may still be missing** — unplayed areas can hide one; the
  dampening-field volumes draw only where retail itself draws them.
- **The GLES 3.2 renderer is selectable, not the default** — Graphics → Renderer
  switches between Vulkan and the GLES 3.2 twin; Vulkan remains the default and
  the renderer the release ships first. (On Linux the release is Vulkan only:
  the AppImage does not carry the GLES 3.2 module.)
- **Saves are one way in practice** — a save written here can load oddly or
  crash in retail, so back your saves up and finish a run in this port.
- **One deliberate divergence** — some scripted movement threads resume in this
  port that retail itself cannot resume from its own saves; this is kept on
  purpose and will not be "fixed".
- **REPORT YOUR ISSUES PLEASE!**

## Build

Everything needed to build is in this repository; no game data is required to
build, only to run.

Android: SDK (`android-35`), NDK `27.0.12077973`, CMake `3.31.5`, JDK 17+,
Gradle 8.13 (wrapper pins it):

```sh
echo "sdk.dir=$HOME/Android/sdk" > local.properties
app/build_apk.sh assembleDebug          # app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk
```

Linux x86-64: CMake 3.31.5+, Ninja, a Vulkan driver:

```sh
cd app/src/main/cpp && cmake --preset linux-gles3 && ninja -C build -j16
```

The Linux output lands in `build/Release/` (`provenanceef2` plus `game.so`,
`cgame.so`, `renderer_vulkan.so`, `renderer_gles3.so`), and reads its game data
from a `base/` directory beside it, or `+set fs_basepath /path/to/install`.

Linux release: `app/package_appimage.sh` builds the engine and packs one
relocatable AppImage, `app/appimage/dist/ProvenanceEF2-<version>-x86_64.AppImage`,
with a `.sha256` and a build-information file beside it. It needs CMake 3.31.5+,
Ninja and the SDL 2 and Vulkan development headers (`libsdl2-dev`,
`libvulkan-dev`); the AppImage tools themselves are downloaded once into
`~/.local/share/appimage-tools` and checked against the pins in
`app/appimage/tools.lock`. The version in the file name comes from
`app/src/main/cpp/VERSION`, the same file the engine banner and the APK read.
The Linux release is **Vulkan only** — the GLES 3.2 twin is an Android concern
and is configured out of this build (`-DBUILD_RENDERER_GLES3=OFF`).

```sh
app/package_appimage.sh
```

The AppImage carries no game data. Run it and point it at your own retail
installation:

```sh
./ProvenanceEF2-<version>-x86_64.AppImage +set fs_basepath /path/to/elite-force-ii
```

or drop the retail `base/` directory into `~/.local/share/provenanceef2/`, which
the engine searches on its own; configs and saves land in the same tree. The
image is bundled with the SDL it was linked against and nothing else from the
desktop — glibc, the X11/Wayland client libraries, ALSA/PulseAudio and the
Vulkan loader and driver all come from the host, so the image needs the build
machine's glibc or newer (that floor is measured and printed, not assumed).
`.github/workflows/appimage.yml` builds it by hand on an Ubuntu 22.04 runner,
which is the same 2.35 floor the EF1 port targets, and uploads it as an
artifact — no release is cut. The pipeline never runs the game: the image is
verified statically (`ldd -r`, `readelf`, `objdump`, `desktop-file-validate`,
`appstreamcli`, and an `--appimage-extract` round trip), and first launch is
yours.

## Licence

Mixed-licence work. The engine lineage is **GPL-2.0-or-later** (id Tech 3 via
ioquake3 and OpenMoHAA — keep the notices intact and ship the source). The Elite
Force II game layer is **not GPL**: the 2003 SDK portions carry Ritual's own
limited-use licence (`docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt`), and the reconstructed
client/UI code asserts no copyright and grants no rights — it remains the property
of its respective holders. **No game assets** are distributed here; you must own
the game ([GOG](https://www.gog.com/en/game/star_trek_elite_force_ii)). Unofficial
fan project, not affiliated with or endorsed by Ritual, Activision, id Software or
Paramount/CBS. See [LICENSING.md](LICENSING.md),
[CREDITS.md](CREDITS.md) and [THIRDPARTY.md](THIRDPARTY.md).

Parts of this port were built with the help of AI tools (model/texture formats,
the bot and NPC AI system, special effects, and engine/client reconstruction).

# Credits

**ProvenanceEF2** is an unofficial, non-commercial port of *Star Trek: Voyager , 
Elite Force II* to Android. It is not affiliated with or endorsed by any rights
holder.

*Star Trek: Voyager, Elite Force II* (2003) was developed by **Ritual
Entertainment** and published by **Activision**. The 2000 predecessor, *Star
Trek: Voyager, Elite Force*, was developed by Raven Software; Raven Software did
not develop the sequel. The Elite Force II SDK was released by Ritual
Entertainment in 2003; its own agreement, dated 2003-10-15, ships with this
repository at `docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt`, and the licence position is
set out in `LICENSING.md`.

## Upstream work this port builds on

- **id Tech 3**, the engine lineage. EF2 shipped a modified id Tech 3; this port
  starts from the de-MOHAAd tree of **OpenMoHAA**, itself derived from id Software's
  Quake III Arena GPL release and from the OpenMoHAA project.
- **Elite Force II SDK** (Ritual Entertainment, 2003), the officially released
  server-side game source for the 1.10 patch, the authority for `fgame`. Used as
  reference and, where it is the shipped source, transcribed verbatim; the SDK's
  per-file banner is kept intact and the Agreement travels with it.
- **The retail Mac PPC binaries**, the primary reverse-engineering reference for
  every body retail did not ship source for; no rights are asserted over the
  material rebuilt from them (see `LICENSING.md`).
- **SDL2**, windowing, input and the audio device layer.
- **OpenAL Soft**, the sound backend.
- **libmad, libogg, libvorbis, opus, opusfile, libxmp**, audio decoding.
- **zlib, libjpeg, freetype**, compression, image and font support.
- **GameSpy SDK**, the multiplayer service glue.

## Art and assets

No retail game assets are distributed. The on-screen touch-control art and the
launcher icon are the port's own.

# Project-wide Gradle settings.

# JVM arguments for the daemon.  The native build (five engine .so's plus SDL2
# and OpenAL Soft) is the heavy part, not gradle; 1536m is what EF1 ships and
# is enough.
org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx1536m

# AndroidX is not used: the activities are plain android.app (EF1's rule too --
# no androidx dependency at all).
android.useAndroidX=false



================================================
FILE: gradlew
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash

##############################################################################
##
##  Gradle start up script for UN*X
##
##############################################################################

# Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS=""

APP_NAME="Gradle"
APP_BASE_NAME=`basename "$0"`

# Use the maximum available, or set MAX_FD != -1 to use that value.
MAX_FD="maximum"

warn ( ) {
    echo "$*"
}

die ( ) {
    echo
    echo "$*"
    echo
    exit 1
}

# OS specific support (must be 'true' or 'false').
cygwin=false
msys=false
darwin=false
case "`uname`" in
  CYGWIN* )
    cygwin=true
    ;;
  Darwin* )
    darwin=true
    ;;
  MINGW* )
    msys=true
    ;;
esac

# Attempt to set APP_HOME
# Resolve links: $0 may be a link
PRG="$0"
# Need this for relative symlinks.
while [ -h "$PRG" ] ; do
    ls=`ls -ld "$PRG"`
    link=`expr "$ls" : '.*-> \(.*\)$'`
    if expr "$link" : '/.*' > /dev/null; then
        PRG="$link"
    else
        PRG=`dirname "$PRG"`"/$link"
    fi
done
SAVED="`pwd`"
cd "`dirname \"$PRG\"`/" >/dev/null
APP_HOME="`pwd -P`"
cd "$SAVED" >/dev/null

CLASSPATH=$APP_HOME/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar

# Determine the Java command to use to start the JVM.
if [ -n "$JAVA_HOME" ] ; then
    if [ -x "$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java" ] ; then
        # IBM's JDK on AIX uses strange locations for the executables
        JAVACMD="$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java"
    else
        JAVACMD="$JAVA_HOME/bin/java"
    fi
    if [ ! -x "$JAVACMD" ] ; then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: $JAVA_HOME

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
else
    JAVACMD="java"
    which java >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
fi

# Increase the maximum file descriptors if we can.
if [ "$cygwin" = "false" -a "$darwin" = "false" ] ; then
    MAX_FD_LIMIT=`ulimit -H -n`
    if [ $? -eq 0 ] ; then
        if [ "$MAX_FD" = "maximum" -o "$MAX_FD" = "max" ] ; then
            MAX_FD="$MAX_FD_LIMIT"
        fi
        ulimit -n $MAX_FD
        if [ $? -ne 0 ] ; then
            warn "Could not set maximum file descriptor limit: $MAX_FD"
        fi
    else
        warn "Could not query maximum file descriptor limit: $MAX_FD_LIMIT"
    fi
fi

# For Darwin, add options to specify how the application appears in the dock
if $darwin; then
    GRADLE_OPTS="$GRADLE_OPTS \"-Xdock:name=$APP_NAME\" \"-Xdock:icon=$APP_HOME/media/gradle.icns\""
fi

# For Cygwin, switch paths to Windows format before running java
if $cygwin ; then
    APP_HOME=`cygpath --path --mixed "$APP_HOME"`
    CLASSPATH=`cygpath --path --mixed "$CLASSPATH"`
    JAVACMD=`cygpath --unix "$JAVACMD"`

    # We build the pattern for arguments to be converted via cygpath
    ROOTDIRSRAW=`find -L / -maxdepth 1 -mindepth 1 -type d 2>/dev/null`
    SEP=""
    for dir in $ROOTDIRSRAW ; do
        ROOTDIRS="$ROOTDIRS$SEP$dir"
        SEP="|"
    done
    OURCYGPATTERN="(^($ROOTDIRS))"
    # Add a user-defined pattern to the cygpath arguments
    if [ "$GRADLE_CYGPATTERN" != "" ] ; then
        OURCYGPATTERN="$OURCYGPATTERN|($GRADLE_CYGPATTERN)"
    fi
    # Now convert the arguments - kludge to limit ourselves to /bin/sh
    i=0
    for arg in "$@" ; do
        CHECK=`echo "$arg"|grep -E -c "$OURCYGPATTERN" -`
        CHECK2=`echo "$arg"|grep -E -c "^-"`                                 ### Determine if an option

        if [ $CHECK -ne 0 ] && [ $CHECK2 -eq 0 ] ; then                    ### Added a condition
            eval `echo args$i`=`cygpath --path --ignore --mixed "$arg"`
        else
            eval `echo args$i`="\"$arg\""
        fi
        i=$((i+1))
    done
    case $i in
        (0) set -- ;;
        (1) set -- "$args0" ;;
        (2) set -- "$args0" "$args1" ;;
        (3) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" ;;
        (4) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" ;;
        (5) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" ;;
        (6) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" "$args5" ;;
        (7) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" "$args5" "$args6" ;;
        (8) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" "$args5" "$args6" "$args7" ;;
        (9) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" "$args5" "$args6" "$args7" "$args8" ;;
    esac
fi

# Split up the JVM_OPTS And GRADLE_OPTS values into an array, following the shell quoting and substitution rules
function splitJvmOpts() {
    JVM_OPTS=("$@")
}
eval splitJvmOpts $DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS $JAVA_OPTS $GRADLE_OPTS
JVM_OPTS[${#JVM_OPTS[*]}]="-Dorg.gradle.appname=$APP_BASE_NAME"

exec "$JAVACMD" "${JVM_OPTS[@]}" -classpath "$CLASSPATH" org.gradle.wrapper.GradleWrapperMain "$@"

FILE: gradlew.bat
================================================
@if "%DEBUG%" == "" @echo off
@rem ##########################################################################
@rem
@rem  Gradle startup script for Windows
@rem
@rem ##########################################################################

@rem Set local scope for the variables with windows NT shell
if "%OS%"=="Windows_NT" setlocal

@rem Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
set DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS=

set DIRNAME=%~dp0
if "%DIRNAME%" == "" set DIRNAME=.
set APP_BASE_NAME=%~n0
set APP_HOME=%DIRNAME%

@rem Find java.exe
if defined JAVA_HOME goto findJavaFromJavaHome

set JAVA_EXE=java.exe
%JAVA_EXE% -version >NUL 2>&1
if "%ERRORLEVEL%" == "0" goto init

echo.
echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.
echo.
echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
echo location of your Java installation.

goto fail

:findJavaFromJavaHome
set JAVA_HOME=%JAVA_HOME:"=%
set JAVA_EXE=%JAVA_HOME%/bin/java.exe

if exist "%JAVA_EXE%" goto init

echo.
echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: %JAVA_HOME%
echo.
echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
echo location of your Java installation.

goto fail

:init
@rem Get command-line arguments, handling Windowz variants

if not "%OS%" == "Windows_NT" goto win9xME_args
if "%@eval[2+2]" == "4" goto 4NT_args

:win9xME_args
@rem Slurp the command line arguments.
set CMD_LINE_ARGS=
set _SKIP=2

:win9xME_args_slurp
if "x%~1" == "x" goto execute

set CMD_LINE_ARGS=%*
goto execute

:4NT_args
@rem Get arguments from the 4NT Shell from JP Software
set CMD_LINE_ARGS=%$

:execute
@rem Setup the command line

set CLASSPATH=%APP_HOME%\gradle\wrapper\gradle-wrapper.jar

@rem Execute Gradle
"%JAVA_EXE%" %DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS% %JAVA_OPTS% %GRADLE_OPTS% "-Dorg.gradle.appname=%APP_BASE_NAME%" -classpath "%CLASSPATH%" org.gradle.wrapper.GradleWrapperMain %CMD_LINE_ARGS%

:end
@rem End local scope for the variables with windows NT shell
if "%ERRORLEVEL%"=="0" goto mainEnd

:fail
rem Set variable GRADLE_EXIT_CONSOLE if you need the _script_ return code instead of
rem the _cmd.exe /c_ return code!
if  not "" == "%GRADLE_EXIT_CONSOLE%" exit 1
exit /b 1

:mainEnd
if "%OS%"=="Windows_NT" endlocal

:omega

FILE: LICENSING.md

# Licensing

This repository is **not** covered by one licence, and it cannot be. It contains
three bodies of source that are governed by different terms, and two of those
terms are mutually exclusive when they meet in a single file. This document
states which terms reach which part, and where the project cannot satisfy a term
it states that too rather than papering over it.

The project is **unofficial**. It is not affiliated with, endorsed by or
sponsored by Ritual Entertainment, Activision, id Software, Raven Software,
Paramount or CBS. It is **non-commercial**. It ships **no game assets**: the
player supplies their own retail installation. It comes with **no warranty**.

## The four provenance buckets

Every native source file under `app/src/main/cpp/code/` carries a notice that
declares one bucket, and `PROVENANCE.tsv` records the bucket, the nearest
ancestor the decision was taken from and the similarity that supported it. The
ledger is generated by `tools/license_notices.py` and checked by
the per-file ledger; the notices are enforced by the project's local license check.

| Bucket | What it is | Notice | SPDX id |
|---|---|---|---|
| GPL lineage | id Tech 3 through OpenMoHAA, including the vendored tree under `code/thirdparty/` | the ancestor's own GNU GPL notice, kept verbatim | `GPL-2.0-or-later` |
| SDK-derived | Elite Force II game logic transcribed from the Ritual EF2 SDK | the SDK's per-file Ritual banner, kept verbatim | `LicenseRef-Ritual-EF2-SDK` |
| Retail reconstruction | engine, cgame, UI and model-layer code the publisher never released as source, rebuilt from the retail binaries | the port asserts no copyright and grants no rights | `LicenseRef-EF2-Retail-Reconstruction` |
| Vendored | a third party's component with its own notice (GameSpy, lcc, zlib, libjpeg, ...) | that party's notice, restored verbatim and never relabelled | (the vendor's own terms; see `THIRDPARTY.md`) |

`code/thirdparty/` keeps the notices it shipped with. Files that carry a
third party's own notice are never given a project SPDX id.

## 1. The engine lineage: GNU GPL v2 or later

The engine, `code/qcommon/`, `code/botlib/`, `code/renderercommon/`,
`code/renderergles3/`, `code/sdl/`, `code/sys/` and `code/thirdparty/`, descends
from id Software's Quake III Arena release, through ioquake3 and OpenMoHAA.
Those files are free software under the GNU General Public License, version 2 or
(at your option) any later version. The full text is `COPYING.txt` at the
repository root. Each file carries the ancestor's own notice, byte for byte, with
`SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0-or-later` added beside it; files the port has
changed also carry a change notice, as GPLv2 section 2(a) requires.

## 2. The Elite Force II game layer: the Ritual SDK agreement

`Star Trek: Voyager, Elite Force II` (2003) was developed by **Ritual
Entertainment** and published by **Activision**. Raven Software developed the
2000 predecessor, *Star Trek: Voyager, Elite Force*; it did not develop the
sequel. In 2003 Ritual released the Elite Force II SDK, server-side game code
for the 1.10 patch, under a Limited Use Software License Agreement. That
agreement is dated 2003-10-15 and is reproduced verbatim in this repository at
[`docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt`](docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt).

The `fgame` module, and the SDK's reusable headers, derive from that SDK. They
are **not** under the GNU GPL and must not be labelled as if they were:

- **§2(e)** forbids commercial exploitation, and **§3(a)** permits distribution
  only free of charge, for non-commercial purposes, by electronic means, for use
  with the full retail game;
- **§2(f)** forbids disassembling, reverse engineering, decompiling or modifying
  the Software, and **§2(j)** forbids preparing derivative works based on it;
- **§2(k)** forbids removing or altering the copyright and licence notices, which
  is why the SDK's per-file banner is restored intact on every SDK-derived file;
- **§8** forbids sublicensing and terminates the licence automatically on breach.

### The two terms this project cannot satisfy

**The SDK agreement's §2(f) and §2(j) cannot be satisfied by this project's own
method, and the project does not pretend otherwise.** §2(f) forbids reverse
engineering and modification of the SDK, and §2(j) forbids derivative works
based on it. The port's game layer is a modified, transcribed, rebuilt body of
code: it is exactly a derivative work, produced in part by reading the retail
binaries. Anyone distributing SDK-derived material from this tree therefore
depends on §3(a)'s New-Creation grant, not on a clean reading of §2(f)/(j), and
§3(a) is itself limited to free, electronic, non-commercial distribution for use
with the full game. If that is not a grant you can rely on, do not distribute the
SDK-derived part.

### The contradiction recorded, not resolved

The SDK ships two notices that do not agree. `licenseAgreement.txt` §3(a)
permits free electronic non-commercial redistribution with the Agreement
attached; the per-file banner says the source *"may not be distributed and/or
modified without expressly written permission by Ritual Entertainment, Inc."*
The package's own `readme.txt` names `licenseAgreement.txt` as the full
agreement, so this project treats the package agreement as the operative grant
and keeps the stricter per-file banner intact as §2(k) requires. It does not
claim that the two can be reconciled.

### The Agreement travels with the source

§3(a) makes the grant conditional on the Agreement accompanying each copy. A copy
is therefore part of this repository at
[`docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt`](docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt), byte
for byte from the SDK package, and any distribution of SDK-derived source from
this tree must include it.

### The indemnity

§6 provides that the licensee indemnifies Ritual and runs to whoever publishes
the SDK-derived material, and it sets Texas law and Dallas County venue. That is
the publishing party's exposure, not Ritual's.

## 3. Rebuilt from the retail binaries: no licence granted

The publisher released no source for the client-game module, the UI layer, the
model/container layer or the 2003 engine's own additions. Those files were
reconstructed from the retail binaries. This project **asserts no copyright in
them and grants no rights to them**: they remain the property of their respective
rights holders, and they are covered neither by the GNU GPL nor by the Ritual SDK
agreement. They carry the statement above and the identifier
`LicenseRef-EF2-Retail-Reconstruction`, which is a marker that no licence is
granted, not a licence.

A file never carries both a GNU GPL notice and a Ritual banner. GPLv2 section 6
forbids imposing further restrictions on recipients, and the SDK agreement
imposes them, so the two cannot govern the same file. Where the provenance of a
file could not be established, the honest treatment is the no-rights statement,
not a licence claim.

## 4. No single licence covers the tree

Because the GPL and the SDK agreement cannot both reach one file, and because
part of the tree is not the project's to license at all, no single identifier
describes the whole repository. A distributor must read `PROVENANCE.tsv` (or the
per-file notice) for each file. The repository root therefore carries no blanket
`LICENSE` file; the engine licence text is `COPYING.txt` and the SDK agreement is
`docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt`.

## Forks and redistribution

If you fork or redistribute this repository or a build made from it:

- keep every notice, banner and SPDX identifier intact;
- ship `docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt` with any SDK-derived material, as
  §3(a) requires;
- keep the engine and the SDK-derived modules as separate programs, joined only
  through the SDK's published interface, so the GPL does not reach the
  SDK-derived code and the SDK restrictions do not reach the GPL code;
- do not ship retail assets, and do not use the material commercially.

## Checks in this repository

- the local license check: every source file carries its provenance notice, and
  no file carries two conflicting notices.
- the local shipped-text check: shipped markdown keeps one impersonal voice, and no
  shipped text names a user home path or a `.cfg` outside the engine's own set.
- `PROVENANCE.tsv`, the per-file ledger, regenerated by the project's local scripts
  and checks it against the ancestor corpora.

// ProvenanceEF2 -- Star Trek: Elite Force II retail-exact decomp port, Android APK.

pluginManagement {
    repositories {
        google()
        mavenCentral()
        gradlePluginPortal()
    }
}

dependencyResolutionManagement {
    repositoriesMode.set(RepositoriesMode.FAIL_ON_PROJECT_REPOS)
    repositories {
        google()
        mavenCentral()
    }
}

rootProject.name = "provenanceef2"
include(":app")



================================================
FILE: THIRDPARTY.md
================================================
# Third-party components

Vendored under `app/src/main/cpp/code/thirdparty/` and wired by
`app/src/main/cpp/cmake/libraries/`.

| Component | Licence |
|---|---|
| SDL2 (2.30.9, 2.32.8) | zlib |
| OpenAL Soft (`openal-soft-1.24.3`) | LGPL-2.1 |
| libmad | GPL-2.0 |
| libogg (`1.3.6`), libvorbis (`1.3.7`) | BSD-3-Clause |
| opus (`1.5.2`), opusfile (`0.12`) | BSD-3-Clause |
| libjpeg (`jpeg-9f`) | IJG |
| zlib (`1.3.1`) | zlib |
| curl (`8.15.0`) | curl |
| GameSpy SDK (`code/gamespy/`) | GameSpy SDK licence |
| libc++ (`app/thirdparty/libcxx-16k/`) | Apache-2.0 with LLVM exception |

The 16 KB-page-aligned `libc++_shared.so` in `app/thirdparty/libcxx-16k/` comes
from NDK r27; its symbol audit is in `app/thirdparty/libcxx-16k/PROVENANCE.md`.

`code/gamespy/`, `code/tools/` and the other vendored directories under
`app/src/main/cpp/code/` keep their own upstream notices, restored verbatim; they
are never relabelled with a project licence identifier. `PROVENANCE.tsv` records
each of them as `vendored`.

The Elite Force II SDK-derived and retail-reconstructed parts of the tree are not
third-party libraries and are not covered by this file: their terms, and the
reason no single licence covers the tree, are in `LICENSING.md`. The SDK
Agreement ships verbatim at `docs/EF2-SDK-licenseAgreement.txt`.



================================================
FILE: app/build.gradle.kts
================================================
plugins {
    id("com.android.application")
}


android {
    namespace = "com.provenanceef2"
    compileSdk = 35
    ndkVersion = "27.0.12077973"

    defaultConfig {
        // MUST match ANDROID_DATA_ROOT in src/main/cpp/cmake/platforms/android.cmake:
        applicationId = "com.provenanceef2"

        minSdk = 26           // Android 8.0, the port's platform-layer floor
        targetSdk = 35        // game data lives in the app's own OBB dir; no storage permission

        // Bumped with the zpak: assets re-extract only when this changes, and
        // global/defaultoptions.cfg lost the cl_renderer row, so an install that keeps
        // the old extracted pak would still re-pin the renderer on Restore Defaults.
        versionCode = 10801
        versionName = file("src/main/cpp/VERSION").readText().trim()
        // The name carries the commit the APK was built from, so two installs are
        // distinguishable in a manifest dump.  versionCode stays the monotone
        // release counter: moving it would re-extract the assets on every build.
        versionNameSuffix = runCatching {
            val sha = providers.exec {
                commandLine("git", "rev-parse", "--short", "HEAD")
            }.standardOutput.asText.get().trim()
            val dirty = providers.exec {
                commandLine("git", "status", "--porcelain")
            }.standardOutput.asText.get().trim()
            "+" + sha + (if (dirty.isEmpty()) "" else "-dirty")
        }.getOrElse { "+nogit" }

        externalNativeBuild {
            cmake {
                arguments += listOf(
                    "-DEF2_PLATFORM=android",
                    "-DEF2_RENDERER=gles3",
                    "-DANDROID_STL=c++_shared",
                    "-DANDROID_PLATFORM=android-26",
                    "-Wno-dev"
                )
                abiFilters += "arm64-v8a"
            }
        }
    }

    signingConfigs {
        getByName("debug") {
            storeFile = file("debug.keystore")
            storePassword = "android"
            keyAlias = "androiddebugkey"
            keyPassword = "android"
        }
        if (project.hasProperty("RELEASE_STORE_FILE")) {
            create("release") {
                storeFile = file(project.property("RELEASE_STORE_FILE") as String)
                storePassword = project.property("RELEASE_STORE_PASSWORD") as String
                keyAlias = project.property("RELEASE_KEY_ALIAS") as String
                keyPassword = project.property("RELEASE_KEY_PASSWORD") as String
            }
        }
    }

    buildTypes {
        getByName("debug") {
            signingConfig = signingConfigs.getByName("debug")
        }
        getByName("release") {
            // -Pef2rd=true: debuggable release for RenderDoc injection (same keystore, install -r safe)
            isDebuggable = project.hasProperty("ef2rd")
            isMinifyEnabled = false
            proguardFiles(getDefaultProguardFile("proguard-android.txt"), "proguard-rules.pro")
            // The shipped APK keeps one channel: the crash backtrace.  This define
            // compiles every probe row family out (CMakeLists EF2_PROBE_RELEASE_
            // SILENT -> qa_probe.h PROBE_BUILD_SILENT), so a device property left
            // at 1 cannot arm them.  -Pef2diag is the one escape: a diagnostic APK
            // (with -Pef2rd) that logs; never shipped.
            externalNativeBuild {
                cmake {
                    if (!project.hasProperty("ef2diag")) {
                        arguments += "-DEF2_PROBE_RELEASE_SILENT=ON"
                    }
                }
            }
            signingConfig = if (project.hasProperty("RELEASE_STORE_FILE")) {
                signingConfigs.getByName("release")
            } else {
                signingConfigs.getByName("debug")
            }
        }
    }

    packaging {
        jniLibs {
            useLegacyPackaging = true
        }
    }

    externalNativeBuild {
        cmake {
            path = file("src/main/cpp/CMakeLists.txt")
            version = "3.31.5"
        }
    }

    lint {
        abortOnError = false
    }

    compileOptions {
        sourceCompatibility = JavaVersion.VERSION_11
        targetCompatibility = JavaVersion.VERSION_11
    }
}

dependencies {
}

val libcxx16k = file("thirdparty/libcxx-16k/arm64-v8a/libc++_shared.so")

val fixLibcxxPageAlignment = tasks.register("fixLibcxxPageAlignment") {
    inputs.file(libcxx16k)
    doLast {
        if (!libcxx16k.isFile) {
            throw GradleException("missing ${libcxx16k} -- the 16 KB-aligned libc++_shared.so (see PROVENANCE.md)")
        }
        var replaced = 0
        val buildRoot = layout.buildDirectory.get().asFile
        android.buildTypes.forEach { bt ->
            val cap = bt.name.replaceFirstChar { it.uppercase() }
            listOf(
                "${buildRoot}/intermediates/merged_native_libs/${bt.name}/merge${cap}NativeLibs/out",
                "${buildRoot}/intermediates/stripped_native_libs/${bt.name}/strip${cap}DebugSymbols/out"
            ).forEach { outRoot ->
                val f = file("${outRoot}/lib/arm64-v8a/libc++_shared.so")
                if (f.isFile) {
                    f.writeBytes(libcxx16k.readBytes())
                    replaced++
                }
            }
        }
        if (replaced == 0) {
            println("fixLibcxxPageAlignment: no libc++_shared.so in the merge outputs (nothing packaged?)")
        } else {
            println("fixLibcxxPageAlignment: replaced ${replaced} libc++_shared.so with the 16 KB-aligned runtime")
        }
    }
}

tasks.whenTaskAdded {
    val t = this
    if (t.name.matches(Regex("strip.*DebugSymbols"))) {
        t.dependsOn(fixLibcxxPageAlignment)
    }
    if (t.name.matches(Regex("merge.*NativeLibs"))) {
        fixLibcxxPageAlignment.configure { mustRunAfter(t) }
    }
}



================================================
FILE: app/build_apk.sh
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ProvenanceEF2 Android APK build (the device target).
#
# Usage:   app/build_apk.sh [assembleDebug|assembleRelease|installDebug] ...
# Default: assembleDebug
#
# The heavy lifting is the engine's own CMake build (app/src/main/cpp), driven by
# gradle's externalNativeBuild with the NDK toolchain; this wrapper sets the
# environment gradle needs and runs it in the foreground.
#
# Requires: an Android SDK (local.properties, or ANDROID_SDK_ROOT) with the
# platform + build-tools + the pinned NDK, and a JDK 17+.
set -euo pipefail

PROJ="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
ROOT="$(cd "$PROJ/.." && pwd)"

# Tree isolation: never let a build absorb an untracked or modified file the engine's
# CMake globs would compile straight into the APK.  The guard fails here instead.
# Deliberate override: EF2_TREE_DIRTY_OK=1
"$ROOT/personal/scripts/check_tree_isolation.sh" --build || exit $?

export ANDROID_SDK_ROOT="${ANDROID_SDK_ROOT:-$HOME/Android/sdk}"
export ANDROID_HOME="$ANDROID_SDK_ROOT"
export JAVA_HOME="${JAVA_HOME:-/usr/lib/jvm/java-21-openjdk-amd64}"

#
# Keep Gradle's and ccache's state inside the tree when $HOME cannot be written.
#
# Both default to $HOME (.gradle / .cache/ccache).  A sandboxed shell is refused
# there ("Read-only file system") and Gradle dies on its wrapper lock before it
# builds anything -- the failure is `gradle-8.13-bin.zip.lck ... Read-only file
# system`, not a build error.  When $HOME is not writable we fall back to copies
# kept under personal/evidence/ (gitignored), so the build completes with no privilege
# escalation at all.  A normal interactive shell has a writable $HOME and is
# completely unaffected -- no path here changes for it.
#
if [ ! -w "${HOME}/.gradle" ] && [ -d "${ROOT}/personal/evidence/gradle-home/wrapper" ]; then
    export GRADLE_USER_HOME="${GRADLE_USER_HOME:-${ROOT}/personal/evidence/gradle-home}"
    echo "note: \$HOME/.gradle is not writable -> GRADLE_USER_HOME=${GRADLE_USER_HOME}"
fi
if [ ! -w "${HOME}/.cache" ] && [ -d "${ROOT}/personal/evidence/ccache" ]; then
    export CCACHE_DIR="${CCACHE_DIR:-${ROOT}/personal/evidence/ccache}"
    echo "note: \$HOME/.cache is not writable -> CCACHE_DIR=${CCACHE_DIR}"
fi
# AGP keeps its analytics/prefs under $HOME/.android and WARNS when that is not
# writable.  Point it at a writable directory inside the tree instead.
# NOTE: do NOT also set ANDROID_PREFS_ROOT.  AGP 8.13 rejects the legacy variable:
# it fails to construct AndroidLocationsBuildService and the whole configure dies.
if [ ! -w "${HOME}/.android" ]; then
    export ANDROID_USER_HOME="${ANDROID_USER_HOME:-${ROOT}/personal/evidence/android-home}"
    mkdir -p "$ANDROID_USER_HOME"
    echo "note: \$HOME/.android is not writable -> ANDROID_USER_HOME=${ANDROID_USER_HOME}"
fi

cd "$ROOT"
./gradlew "${@:-assembleDebug}"
